## The King's Hall: Youzhou
<note>Youzhou, the "Hidden Province," corresponds to the northeast, including the area of modern Beijing and Hebei. In Taiyi divination, this sector is known as the "King's Hall."</note>

<margin>According to the "Tribute of Yu" <note>original: *Yu Gong*; an ancient geographical treatise.</note>, this was within the territory of Jizhou. When the sage-emperor Shun established the Twelve Pastures <note>The legendary administrative divisions of ancient China.</note>, this was one of them. The *Rites of Zhou* <note>original: *Zhou Li*</note> states that this is the northeast region.</margin>

Regarding Youzhou, the *Spring and Autumn: Primordial Mandate of Fate* <note>original: *Chunqiu Yuan Ming Bao*; an apocryphal text that links terrestrial geography to celestial bodies.</note> says that the essence of the Sieve star <note>original: *Jixing*; the seventh mansion of the Azure Dragon of the East, associated with wind.</note> scattered to become Youzhou, which was then divided to form the State of Yan. It is said that because the North represents the "Great Yin" <note>original: *Taiyin*; the extreme of Yin energy, associated with winter, water, and darkness.</note>, the province was named "You" <note>The character *You* (幽) translates to hidden, dark, or the underworld, reflecting the province's northern, "shadowy" position.</note>.

King Wu of the Zhou dynasty enfeoffed the Duke of Shao at Yan. When the Qin state destroyed Yan, they converted the territory into the commanderies of Yuyang and Shanggu. Emperor Gaozu of the Han dynasty divided Shanggu to establish Zhuo Commandery. Later, Emperor Wu established the Thirteen Provinces, and it was once again named Youzhou.

Fanzhou, Yanzhou, Wanping, Guangning, Dai Commandery, and Liaodong <note>A list of administrative seats and regions within the Youzhou jurisdiction during the Tang and Song eras.</note>.

# The Great One’s Classic of Blessed Response: Volume 9

## 96. Explaining the Movements and Stillness of Enemy Nations

The Classic says: Those who wish to know the movements or stillness of an enemy nation should regularly use the Calculator Spirit <term>Jishen; a mathematical spirit in Taiyi divination used to calculate timing and the "intent" of a situation</term> to decide the matter. If the Guest Calculation <term>Kesuan; the numerical value representing the opponent or the "Guest"</term> results in the configurations of "Stake and Blockage," "No Gate," or if the General is set to "Deploy without Concealment," "Pressed Against the Pass," "Prisoner Grid," or "Direct Confrontation" <note>These are technical terms for specific mathematical remainders in the Taiyi system that indicate vulnerability or imminent action.</note>, then the enemy will come. 

If both the Host <note>The "Host" represents the home nation or the practitioner’s side.</note> and the Guest meet before the Great One <term>Taiyi; the supreme cosmic deity and the central moving point of the divination board</term>, then this is what is called "Universal Auspiciousness." In such a case, the enemy comes only to surrender and does not act as a bandit. If the divination concerns a person from the north, and the Sky Eye <term>Tianmu; an astrological marker used to track the "vision" or direction of events</term> numerical value turns toward the south, they will arrive; if the number turns back toward the north, they will not come.

For example, in the 52nd station of the Yin Hidden Cycle <term>Yindun; the half of the year following the Summer Solstice when Yin energy grows</term>, at the hour of the Wood Rabbit <note>original: *Yimao*</note>, the Great One is in the 8th Palace. The Yin Virtue star serves as the Sky Eye, and the Earth Snake <note>original: *Jisi*</note> is the Calculator Spirit. The First Strike <term>Shiji; a factor representing aggression, sudden movement, or the "spearhead" of an army</term> oversees the Great Cluster <note>original: *Dacu*; the musical pitch-pipe corresponding to the first month of spring and the direction of the Tiger</note>. The Guest Calculation is a single 7. The Guest Great General is in the 7th Palace, the Assistant General is in the 1st Palace, and the Five Generals are all deployed. Yin and Yang are in harmony. Host and Guest both meet in the North, and the number turns toward the North. In this case, the enemy comes to surrender and does not act as a thief.

## 97. Explaining the Truth or Falsehood of Enemy Intelligence

The Classic says: To know whether enemy intelligence is true or false, one must observe what the Great One controls and decide based on the hour. If the Great One is able to control the Guest Eye <note>The Sky Eye (Tianmu) as it pertains to the Guest's position.</note> or the Guest Great General, then the words of the enemy's messenger are true and can be believed. If the Guest Eye overcomes the Great One’s palace, the enemy’s words are false and cannot be trusted.

For instance, in the 25th station of the Yang Hidden Cycle <term>Yangdun; the half of the year following the Winter Solstice when Yang energy grows</term>, at the hour of the Earth Rat <note>original: *Wuzi*</note>, the Great One is in the 1st Palace. The Earth Lord serves as the Sky Eye, and the Tiger <note>original: *Yin*</note> is the...

Because Metal controls Wood, the Guest is restrained. In this case, the words of the enemy’s envoy are sincere and can be trusted.

## 98. Explaining Whether an Enemy Nation Has Spies or Intrigues

The Classic says: Those who wish to know if an enemy nation has spies or secret intrigues should use the Sky Eye <term>Tianmu; an astrological marker used to track the "vision" or focus of events</term> at that time to decide the matter. 

For example, if the Great One is in Palace 1, then *Dacu* <note>The first pitch-pipe, corresponding to the first month of the lunar year</note> serves as the Sky Eye. From *Dacu* to the Great Righteousness position is the "Forward-Retreat" range. From *Dacu* moving through Palaces 7, 2, and 9 to the right is considered "Forward-Inner." From the Great Righteousness moving toward Palaces 8, 3, and 4 as it moves away is considered "Left," "Rear," and "Outer." If the Sky Eye lands on the Yin Lord <note>original: "Yinzhu," a position on the board associated with hidden or internal matters</note>, it means the enemy has entered our borders, and one should investigate with great care.

For example, in the second station of the Yang Hidden Cycle <term>Yangdun; the active half of the divinatory year</term>, during an *Yichou* hour, the Great One is in Palace 1 and *Dacu* is the Sky Eye. The hour sign *Chou* becomes the Calculator Spirit. The Initial Strike lands on the Yin Lord. The Guest Equivalent is a Single-One <note>A specific mathematical result in Taiyi calculations</note>. The Great General is in Palace 1. In this configuration, both the "Two Eyes" <note>The Sky Eye and the Earth Eye</note> and the Great General meet before the Great One in the "Inner" area. In such a case, the enemy’s generals and troops have already entered our territory. One should prepare defenses to guard against them.

## 99. Explaining the Direction from Which the Enemy Comes

The Classic says: When dust rises and beacon fires are lit, and one asks whether enemy troops are present and from which direction they come: if one obtains a "Yang" result, there are bandits; if one obtains...

<summary>This page describes how to use Taiyi divination to determine if an enemy envoy is telling the truth and how to detect the presence of spies or invading troops based on the position of the "Sky Eye" and "Great General" markers.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the analysis of enemy spies and scouts from the previous section and begins Chapter 100, which provides ritual and tactical instructions for troop deployment based on the mathematical values derived from the Taiyi cosmic board.</meta>

If the result is Yin, there are no thieves or spies. In the calculations for Yang-Odd and Yin-Even, if the nine-count <note>A specific method of modular arithmetic used in Taiyi divination</note> reaches sixteen or higher, Yin and Yang are in harmony. This signifies that the enemy will come, accompanied by generals and a great many troops. If the count is fifteen or higher <note>In this context, likely indicating a threshold just below "harmony" or a specific numerical range</note>, the enemies are few and lack both generals and rank-and-file soldiers. If the Sky Eye <term>Tianmu; a mathematical pointer used to determine the focus of a situation</term> lands on the Left, the enemy comes from the East; if it lands on the Right, the enemy comes from the West. If the calculation yields the number five, the way is obstructed and no gates are open; the enemy will not come.

For example: During the eighth configuration of the Yang Shielding <term>Yangdun; the half of the year when the active "Yang" energy is increasing</term>, at the *Xinwei* hour <note>A specific hour in the sexagenary cycle</note>, a divination is made. The Great One <term>Taiyi; the supreme deity and moving point on the board</term> is in Palace 3. Because this is a Yang-Odd palace, it indicates the presence of enemies. The Yang Virtue <note>An auspicious mathematical spirit</note> serves as the Sky Eye. The hour-sign *Wei* serves as the Calculator Spirit <term>Jishen; a spirit used to determine timing and intent</term>. The Initial Strike <term>Shiji; a star representing the attacker’s vanguard</term> lands on the Great Martial position <note>original: "Dawu," a fixed position on the board</note>. The Guest Calculation is twenty-two. Since Yin and Yang are in harmony, the enemy will definitely come, and with generals and soldiers, they will be numerous. The Guest Eye <note>The Sky Eye relative to the "Guest" or invading party</note> is on the Right, meaning they approach from the West.

### 100. Explaining Taiyi Troop Deployment

The Classic says: When deploying troops according to the Great One, take the calculated number of the Great One's current position and use it as a guide for your formation.

If the Great One’s calculation yields **one**:
When deploying the army, the infantry should be in the front, followed by the chariots and cavalry. The Great General stays in the middle. The march must be conducted without the noise of drums. The camp should be situated in the position corresponding to "One." A sacrifice should be made to the West, consisting of one piece of dried beef, two cups of wine, and a mat made of black silk fabric. The Great General should face the center from the West, bow twice, and recite the following incantation:

> "Great One of the First Palace, Ten Thousand Spirits,
> Protect my three armies, and let no enemy dare approach."

If the Great One’s calculation yields **two**:

# Explaining the Initial Strike General's Entry into the Twenty-Eight Lunar Mansions, Number 101

The classic states: The Initial Strike General <term>Shiji Jiang; a specific celestial spirit in Taiyi divination used to determine the timing and direction of an attack</term> is the essence of the "Sparkling Deluder" <term>Yinghuo; the traditional name for the planet Mars, associated with war and fire</term>. It moves according to the annual calculations, entering the twenty-eight mansions <term>Ershiba She; the lunar mansions or constellations of the Chinese zodiac</term>. Throughout the year, it governs the essential omens in each of its dwelling places.

### Chapter 30, Part 2<unclear>22</unclear>: Explanation of the Great One's Upper and Lower Eyes Meeting the Great Yin <term>Taiyin; the Moon or the principle of Yin, used in divination to track shadow-cycles and hidden influences</term>

**The Seven Mansions of the East** <note>These represent the Azure Dragon of the East in Chinese cosmography.</note>
*   **When approaching the Horn:** Great officials have cause for worry, and the army shall march out.
*   **When approaching the Neck:** The common people will suffer from infectious diseases <note>original: "zhuji," referring to chronic, wasting, or epidemic illnesses</note>.
*   **When approaching the Root:** There are rebellious ministers both within the court and in the outer provinces.
*   **When approaching the Tail:** There is worry and disaster within the Inner Palace <note>The living quarters of the Empress and concubines</note>.
*   **When approaching the Winnowing Fan:** War breaks out, and the rank-and-file soldiers become wandering refugees.

**The Seven Mansions of the North** <note>These represent the Black Tortoise of the North, associated with winter and water.</note>
*   **When approaching the Dipper:** Loss of noble rank and salary; mountain passes and bridges <note>original: "guanliang," essential infrastructure for trade and military movement</note> are blocked.
*   **When approaching the Ox:** Grain becomes expensive; the people starve; many of the six domestic animals die.
*   **When approaching the Girl:** The Empress and concubines suffer from disasters or illness.
*   **When approaching the Emptiness:** Various envoys receive new orders, or perhaps there is an imperial amnesty.
*   **When approaching the Rooftop:** A year of many diseases; the soldiers rise in rebellion.
*   **When approaching the Encampment:** Great officials engage in secret plots; the harvest for the year is not gathered.
*   **When approaching the Wall:** Those in charge of literary affairs and those presiding over the military are dismissed.

**The Seven Mansions of the West** <note>These represent the White Tiger of the West, associated with autumn and metal/punishment.</note>
*   **When approaching the Stride:** Low-ranking ministers commit acts of treachery.
*   **When approaching the Bond:** Bandits and thieves appear at the foot of the mountains; the roads become impassable.
*   **When approaching the Stomach:** The five grains do not ripen; warfare occurs.
*   **When approaching the Hairy Head:** Prominent people fall ill; war breaks out in the regions of Yan and Zhao <note>Ancient states in Northern China, often used to denote specific geographic territories</note>.
*   **When approaching the Net:** There is continuous warfare; punishments and executions are frequent.
*   **When approaching the Turtle Beak:** Generals rebel; cattle and horses die in great numbers.
*   **When approaching the Three Stars:** The Imperial Palace is moved; the generals will have cause for worry.

**The Seven Mansions of the South** <note>These represent the Vermillion Bird of the South, associated with summer and fire.</note>
*   **When approaching the Well:** Executions by hanging occur; minor military movements.
*   **When approaching the Ghost:** The Empress and concubines lose their influence; many people fall ill.
*   **When approaching the Willow:** Famine and the flight of refugees.
*   **When approaching the Star:** Worry regarding disasters caused by fire.
*   **When approaching the Extended Net:** Rites and righteousness are bartered away <note>original: "jiaoyi," implying a corruption of social and moral values</note>.
*   **When approaching the Wings:** Yin and Yang lose their natural order; there is excessive rainfall and flooding.
*   **When approaching the Chariot:** There are disasters and funerals.

## Chapter 103: Explanation of the Clashes between the Great One and the Great Year <term>Taisui; the personification of the Jupiter cycle, representing the energy of the current year</term>

The Classic says: When the Great Year "clashes" <term>Ge; a state of astrological gridlock or confrontation between two major celestial bodies</term> with the Great One, it portends the death of rulers, military upheavals, and forced labor or conscription.

When the Great One is in the Third Palace and the Great Year is in the Southwest <note>original: "Wei and Shen," the signs of the Goat and Monkey</note>, a comet will appear in the Northeast to assist the Great Year. The states in the Southwest will be defeated first, followed by those in the Northeast. If the comet appears in the Southwest instead, the order of defeat is reversed. Both scenarios signify wandering refugees and epidemics. This corresponds to the "Exchange of Qi" <note>Yi Qi; a technical term for the shifting or displacement of elemental energy</note>.

When the Great One is in the Fourth Palace and the Great Year is in the West <note>original: "You," the sign of the Rooster</note>, the planet Venus <term>Taibai; the "Great White" planet, associated with metal, autumn, and military execution</term> will appear in the West to assist the Great Year. The eastern states will be defeated first, followed by the western states. If the planet Jupiter <term>Suixing; the "Year Star," associated with wood, spring, and the East</term> appears in the East, the outcome is reversed. Both situations lead to refugees, warfare, and disease. This corresponds to the "Earthly Qi."

When the Great One is in the Eighth Palace and the Great Year is in the South <note>original: "Wu," the sign of the Horse</note>, the planet Mars <term>Yinghuo; the "Sparkling Deluder," associated with fire, summer, and impending calamity</term> will appear in the South to assist the Great Year. The northern states will be defeated first, followed by the southern states. If Mercury <term>Chenxing; the "Hour Star," associated with water, winter, and the North</term> appears in the North, the outcome is reversed, signifying war and death.

When the Great One is in the Nine Palace and the Great Year is in the Northwest <note>original: "Xu and Hai"</note>, a comet will appear in the West to assist the Great Year. The states in the Southeast will be defeated first. If the comet appears in the Southeast, the outcome is reversed, portending war and epidemics. These clashes involve the Third, Eighth, Fourth, and Ninth Palaces and are determined by the position of the Great Year through the signs of the South, Southwest, West, and Northwest.

# The Classic of the Great One’s Blessed Response, Scroll 10

## Section 144: Divining the Emperor’s Imperial Inspection Tours

> The Classic says: If one wishes to know the year in which the Emperor will undertake an imperial inspection tour <term>xunshou; a ritual tour by the sovereign to inspect the realm and perform sacrifices</term>, one must observe the position of the Celestial Eye <term>Tianmu; a divinatory star or mathematical point in the Taiyi system used to determine timing and direction</term>. When it resides in the years of the Four Dimensions <term>siwei; the four corner directions: Northeast, Southeast, Southwest, and Northwest</term>, that will be the period for the Emperor’s inspection tour. If it does not fall there, he will instead dispatch envoys to investigate local customs and morale. To know which direction the Emperor will set out toward, look to the direction directly opposite the location of the Master Eye <term>Zhumu; another primary divinatory point in Taiyi used to represent the host or the central authority</term>. If he sets out to the west, the influence resides in the Great Martial <term>Dawu; a directional spirit or palace associated with the West</term>. If he sets out to the east, it resides in Harmonious Virtue <term>Hede; associated with the East</term>. If he sets out to the south, it resides in Great Radiance <term>Dajiong; associated with the South</term>. If he sets out to the north, it resides in Hidden Virtue <term>Yinde; associated with the North</term>.

->Mirror of the Heart<- <note>original: "Xinjing," likely referring to a specific divinatory formula or a mnemonic guide</note>

Suppose the Celestial Eye is in Hidden Virtue [North]: he sets out to the east when it is in Harmonious Virtue; he sets out to the south when it is in Great Radiance; he sets out to the west when it is in Great Martial; and he sets out to the north when it returns to Hidden Virtue.

## Section 155: Divining the Recommendation of the Worthy and Good from Neighboring States

> The Classic says: If one wishes to know when neighboring states will recommend men of worth, integrity, and moral righteousness, one must examine the Yearly Calculations. If the factors of Heaven, Earth, and Man <term>tiandiren; the "Three Talents" or foundational components of a Taiyi calculation</term> are all complete, then that prefecture or state will certainly recommend such men. It is also said: even if Heaven, Earth, and Man are all present, the recommendation will only occur during a year of the Triple Harmony of the Boar, Rabbit, and Sheep <term>sanhe; a specific astrological grouping where three zodiac signs combine to form a powerful elemental influence</term>. Furthermore, if the Yearly Calculation reaches a sum of ten or higher and Heaven, Earth, and Man are complete, the recommendation proceeds. If the sum is a single digit of nine or below, it is considered incomplete <note>This suggests a mathematical threshold in the Taiyi counting system where higher numbers signify fulfillment or "fullness" of an event</note>.

# The Great One’s Method for Selecting Days, Section 166

The Classic says: There are two methods for selecting auspicious days. The "Lunar Nine Earth" days benefit the Guest <term>Ke; in Taiyi divination, the 'Guest' represents the active party, such as an attacking army or the person initiating a request</term>, while the "Lunar Nine Heaven" days benefit the Host <term>Zhu; the 'Host' represents the defending party or the established authority</term>. However, these methods do not reflect the true meaning of the *Original Classic of the Great One*. I, your servant Weide, and my colleagues have edited these out and do not use them. Instead, we use the "Day Count" <term>Riji; a calculation of the Great One’s position based on a sixty-day cycle</term> method of the Great One. One must observe in which Era, Cycle, and Palace the Great One resides to determine the "Upper and Lower Eyes" <term>Shangxia Ermu; two critical mathematical points in a Taiyi chart used to calculate the relative strength and strategies of the Host and Guest</term> as the primary basis for determining the Host and Guest's positions. 

If one finds that the "Three Gates" <term>Sanmen; the three auspicious 'gates' of Opening, Rest, and Life</term> are fully present and the "Five Generals" <term>Wujiang; the primary celestial spirits representing the Commander, the Assistant, and the various vanguards in a Taiyi chart</term> are in their active positions, while Yin and Yang are in harmony and there is no "Masking, Oppressing, Barring, or Striking" <term>Yan, Po, Guan, Ji; four specific types of celestial interference where the mathematical points 'clash' or 'overlap' inauspiciously</term>, then it is favorable for mobilizing troops, beginning construction, or conducting business. In such cases, all matters will be auspicious. If the conditions are the opposite of this, the day is unfavorable.

# The Great One’s Method for Selecting Hours, Section 177

Wang Ximing <note>A prominent astronomer and scholar of the early 10th century whose works on Taiyi were well-known but sometimes contested by later Song Dynasty scholars</note> said: "The methods for selecting auspicious hours were traditionally established with separate calculations for the sovereign, the heir apparent, the prime minister, the various princes, consorts, and commoners." 

I, your servant, believe that such statements are like "gluing the tuning pegs of a zither" <note>original: "jiaozhu," a classical idiom describing someone who is stubborn, inflexible, or lacks a fundamental understanding of the underlying principles</note>; this is not the reasoning of a true scholar. Therefore, I have deleted those methods and do not record them here. 

In our current method for selecting hours, we simply use the "Hour Count" <term>Shiji; the position of the Great One as it shifts every two-hour period (shi)</term>. If the Gates are complete, the Generals are active, Yin and Yang are in harmony, and there are no instances of "Masking, Oppressing, Barring, or Imprisonment," then it is an auspicious hour. It may be used for military campaigns, construction, and all general affairs to great benefit. If one uses this method to select a time for action, the principles will be consistent and the meaning clear for any "Great Man" <term>Daren; a person of high character, wisdom, or social standing</term> who seeks to act in accordance with the Way.

### Section 109: Divining Calamitous Encounters Based on the Year and City Name

The Classic says: To know of calamitous encounters in states and prefectures, always add the *Lü-Shen* <term>Lü-Shen; specific technical calculation coordinates or spirits used to track movement across the sexagenary cycle in Taiyi divination</term> to the Year-City name. In the city where the Sun <term>Taiyang</term> arrives, that state shall suffer the calamity of military disturbances and forced labor. Where the Yin Lord <term>Yinzhu; a celestial point acting as the polar opposite to the Sun, often associated with internal or "hidden" matters like disease</term> arrives at *Mao* <term>the 4th Earthly Branch, associated with the East and Spring</term>, that state shall suffer the calamity of disasters in *Mao* years. 

Li Chunfeng <note>A renowned Tang dynasty mathematician, astronomer, and historian (602–670 AD) whose works were foundational to Chinese state divination.</note> says: First add *Lü-Shen* to the Heavenly Stem of the Year-City name; then add *Lü-Shen* to the Earthly Branch of the Year-City name, and combine them for the deduction.

Suppose the state of Jingzhou <note>An ancient Chinese province covering parts of modern-day Hubei and Hunan.</note> corresponds to the cycle position *Dingwei* <term>the 44th combination of the sixty-year cycle</term>. By adding *Lü-Shen* to *Dingwei*, the Sun arrives at *Xin* and the Yin Lord arrives at *Yi*. Next, by adding *Lü-Shen* to the branch *Wei*, the Sun arrives at *You* and the Yin Lord arrives at *Mao*. Therefore, in the years of *Xin* and *You*, there will be military disturbances and labor; in the years of *Yimao* and *Yiyou*, there will be epidemics.

Suppose Jingzhou corresponds to *Dingwei*. Its Sun arrives at *You*; thus, in *You* years, the state has the calamity of military disturbances and labor. Its Yin Lord arrives at *Mao*; thus, in *Mao* years, it has the calamity of epidemics. This is how it works.

<summary>This page explains how to predict regional disasters, such as military unrest and epidemics, by calculating the interaction between celestial markers (the Great One, Sun, and Yin Lord) and the specific sexagenary signs assigned to different provinces.</summary>
<keywords>Taiyi, Li Chunfeng, Jingzhou, military unrest, epidemics, celestial divination, disaster prediction, Heavenly Eye, Yin Lord</keywords>

# Section 120: The Four Flying Killings

The Classic says: If you are attacking a city, and the name of that city aligns with the **Four Flying Killings** <term>Feixing Sisha; four specific malign celestial points used to predict military slaughter</term>, there will inevitably be a response in the form of a "massacred city" <note>This refers to the total defeat and potential destruction of the city's inhabitants.</note>. 

One should add the **Yin Master** <term>Yin Zhu; a calculated technical point in the Taiyi system representing hidden or negative forces</term> to the City Name. Below the **Earth Master** <term>Di Zhu; a point representing the terrestrial or defensive force</term> lies the **Disaster Killing** <term>Zai Sha; a star of catastrophe</term>. 

Furthermore, add the **Harmony Spirit** <term>He Shen; a harmonizing numerical factor</term> to the City Name. Below the Disaster Killing lies the **Ghost Killing** <term>Gui Sha</term> and the **Month Killing** <term>Yue Sha</term>, as well as **Heavenly Failure** <term>Tian Bai</term>. 

<gloss>Regarding the killings: In the First Month of the Yi-Si year, on the second day, this was verified; in the first month of a season, use the "You" branch, in the middle month use the "Mao" branch.</gloss> 

Below the Yin Master lies the **Heavenly Official** <term>Tian Li; a technical point often associated with legal or bureaucratic punishment</term>. 

It is also said: Always add the Yin Master to the Year, Month, Day, and Hour coordinates. Below the Earth Master lies the Disaster Killing. Furthermore, add the Harmony Spirit of the Year, Month, Day, and Hour to the Yin Master. Below the Disaster Killing lies the Ghost Killing, and below the Month Killing lies Heavenly Failure. Below the **Yin Killing** <term>Yin Sha</term> lies the Heavenly Official. 

As for the Yin Killing: in the first month, its position arises at the *Yin* <term>the 3rd Earthly Branch</term> position and moves counter-clockwise through the six **Yang Branches**. It is also said: in the first month it arises at *Hai* <term>the 12th Earthly Branch</term> and moves counter-clockwise through the six **Yin Branches**.

<summary>This page maps the traditional Nine Provinces of China to specific calendrical coordinates and explains the "Four Flying Killings," a set of technical calculations used in military divination to predict whether an attacked city will face total destruction.</summary>
<keywords>Taiyi system, Nine Provinces, Four Flying Killings, military astrology, Yin Master, Earth Master, geographic correspondences, Song Dynasty divination</keywords>

### Section 122: Divining the Truth or Falsehood of Information Heard or Seen

The Classic says: When news is heard, use the specific hour of the event to perform the calculation. If the **Heavenly Eye** <term>Tianmu; an auxiliary star in the Taiyi system representing the observer's vision</term> is in a state of **Concealment or Pressure** <term>Yanpo; a technical configuration where stars overlap or are restricted</term> while the **Great One** <term>Taiyi; the primary star representing the cosmic pivot</term> is "Open," then bad news is true (real) and good news is false (empty).

If the **Gates** <term>divinatory positions</term> are fully prepared and the **General** <term>a technical term for a specific numerical factor in the calculation</term> is ready to set out, then an "Open" result that is auspicious will be truly lucky, but an "Open" result that is inauspicious will not result in harm. 

However, if the configuration is "Four Walls Closed and Surrounded," then even an auspicious "Open" result will not bring luck, and an inauspicious result will be truly disastrous.

The Classic says: **Double Yang** <term>Chongyang; a calculation where the numerical totals result in a surplus of odd numbers</term> indicates doubled good fortune. **Double Yin** <term>Chongyin; a surplus of even numbers</term> indicates doubled misfortune. All matters are decided by combining the counts of these doubled Yin and Yang factors.

### Section 123: Divining Whether Distant Persons or Bandits are Coming

The Classic says: If the **Host Count** <term>Zhushu; representing the diviner or the home side</term> is in the South and the **Guest Count** <term>Keshu; representing the person or force approaching</term> is also in the South, this is a case where the "Guest" has obtained the "Host's" position. They will not come.

One method states: 
If the Host expects someone from the **North**, and the Guest count results in Northern coordinates, they will not come; if the Guest count results in Southern coordinates, they will come. 
If configurations such as **Concealment, Pressure, Open, or Prisoner-Grid** <note>These are technical "patterns" or *Ge* in Taiyi mathematics that indicate obstruction.</note> occur, the person has not yet set out.

Another method:
*   When looking for someone from the **North**: If the calculation results in 3 or 8, they will not come; if it results in 2 or 7, they will come.
*   When looking for someone from the **East**: If the calculation results in 4 or 9, they will not come; if it results in 1 or 6, they will come.
*   When looking for someone from the **West**: If the calculation results in 6 or 1, they will not come; if it results in 4 or 9, they will come.
*   When looking for someone from the **South**: If the calculation results in 2 or 7... <note>The text likely continues to explain that 2/7 would mean they do not come, following the logic of the Five Phases' overcoming and production cycles.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the geographic mapping of the stems and branches before providing technical rules for two types of divination: determining if rumors are true and predicting the arrival of travelers or enemies based on numerical counts and star positions.</summary>
<keywords>Taiyi divination, Field Allocation (Fenye), Heavenly Eye, Truth and Falsehood, Bandits, Host and Guest Counts, Yin and Yang, Numerology</keywords>

--- Page 113 ---
<meta>This page continues the practical divinatory applications of the Great One (Taiyi) system. The first section concludes the previous page's guidance on predicting the arrival time of travelers based on numerical results. The text then shifts to legal matters (imprisonment) and social interactions (visiting nobles and making requests).</meta>

If the traveler has not yet arrived but the calculation yields the numbers three or eight, then they will certainly come. One should judge the timing based on the specific number obtained: if the calculation results in one, they will arrive within one day, or at the latest, ten days. If the number is five, they will arrive in two days. If the number is three, they will arrive in three days. Follow this logic for the remaining numbers. When the count is exhausted, the traveler will arrive immediately.

<column-break/>

# Section 124: Determining Matters of Imprisonment

<column-break/>

The Classic says: When the **Heavenly Eye** <term>Tianmu; a star representing the observer's vision and discernment</term> is in a state of **Concealment or Pressure** <term>Yanpo; a technical configuration where stars overlap or are restricted</term> against the **Great One** <term>Taiyi; the supreme cosmic pivot</term>, and the **Host** <term>Zhuren; representing the self or the party being divined for</term> is positioned "outside" while the **Guest** <term>Ke; representing the external party or the authorities</term> stands with a **Prosperous Deity** <term>Wangshen; a star in its most powerful seasonal state</term>, these forces overcome one another to produce an auspicious result. In such a case, it is appropriate to face the officials; one will be released from prison and the matter will be easily resolved. 

However, it is also said: if the **Host** is "inside" and stands with a **Prosperous Deity**, the situation is entirely inauspicious; one must not enter prison or face the officials. 

It is further said: if the **Great One** has only just moved to a new "palace" <note>A "palace" refers to one of the eight or sixteen divisions of the Taiyi compass.</note> on that day, entering prison will lead to certain delays and the situation will be difficult to resolve. If the **Great One** and the **Host** occupy the same palace and the **Heavenly Eye** visits them, entering prison on that day will lead to a quick release. One will encounter a person of high rank <note>original: "Gui" (貴); a noble or influential official</note> who can resolve their worries and suffering. If the calculation yields a number of sixteen or higher, the trouble will also be resolved.

<column-break/>

# Section 125: Divining Visits, Seeking Audiences, and Making Requests

<column-break/>

The Classic says: If the calculation shows the **Heavenly Eye** is "Inside," it is auspicious. One may seek an audience, visit a person of high rank, or seek wealth; in all these matters, one will succeed. If the **Heavenly Eye** is "Outside," or in the positions immediately preceding or following it—where the front is considered "Inside" and the back is "Outside"—and the calculation yields "Outside," this is a state of **Solitude** <term>Gu; an isolated or unsupported position</term>. If "Inside" is **Void** <term>Xu; empty or lacking substance</term>, then on that day, the **Host** is constrained and the **Guest** is unavailable; one should not seek audiences. 

If seeking an audience in the North, all will be successful. If the **Host** is "Inside," one may encounter deception; if "Outside," nothing will be obtained. It is also said: if the calculation shows the **Heavenly Eye** is "Inside," the person you are visiting will be at home.

<summary>This page provides specific divinatory rules for predicting the arrival of travelers, the outcome of legal imprisonment, and the success of social visits or financial requests using the Taiyi system.</summary>
<keywords>Taiyi, Heavenly Eye, imprisonment, legal divination, seeking audiences, Host and Guest, social etiquette, Song Dynasty divination</keywords>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the divinatory instructions for social interactions and legal petitions. It concludes the advice on seeking favors and visiting influential figures before transitioning into specific protocols for law enforcement (capturing fugitives) and dealing with detention or official constraints.</meta>

In seeking and asking for favors, all things will be obtained, and it is beneficial to see "great men" <note>This refers to social superiors, officials, or those with the power to grant requests.</note>. However, if the **Heavenly Eye** <term>Tianmu; a star representing the observer's vision and discernment</term> is positioned "outside," one will visit people but fail to see them, and what is sought will not be accomplished. 

If the **Heavenly Eye** is in a state of **Clashing** <term>Ge; a configuration of opposition or intersection</term> with the **Great One** <term>Taiyi; the supreme cosmic pivot</term>, it is suitable to see nobles and visit for petitions or lawsuits. If the **Host** <term>Zhuren; representing the self or the party being divined for</term> stands with a **Prosperous Deity** <term>Wangshen; a star in its most powerful seasonal state</term>, one must not request an audience or seek to see nobles. In the seasons of spring and summer, if one obtains the number six, and in autumn and winter, the number four, these results are both favorable for use.

## Section 126: Divining the Capture of Rebels and Fugitives

The Classic says: When pursuing and capturing rebels or fugitives, one must use the **Time Calculation** <term>Shiji; a method of determining the timing of events based on the hour</term> to decide the outcome. If the **Guest** <term>Ke; representing the external party or the authorities</term> clashes with the **Host**, or if the **Lower Eye** <term>Xiamu; a secondary calculation point used to determine terrestrial or hidden matters</term> is "inside," or if the **Great One** resides in the same "palace" <term>Gong; one of the eight or sixteen divisions of the Taiyi divinatory board</term> as the **Host**, or if the **Heavenly Eye** also approaches that position, or if the **Heavenly Eye** is "inside," all these configurations signify that the capture will be successful. 

Positions "before" the **Heavenly Eye** are considered "inside," and positions "after" it are considered "outside." Another teaching states: if the **Host** and the **Heavenly Eye** are both "outside," the fugitives will not be caught. It is further said: if the **Guest Eye** and the **Great One** are both in the second palace, the fugitive will be caught but then lost again. 

To search for those hidden in secret and capture them, one must know where they are hiding; if the **Great One** approaches that location and carries the state of **Concealment or Pressure** <term>Yanpo; technical configurations where stars overlap or are restricted</term>, the pursuit will be successful and they will certainly be seized. However, do not go to a place that is in a **Prosperous or Prime** <term>Wangxiang; states where the elemental energy of a location is at its seasonal peak</term> phase; to go there would lead to humiliation and defeat.

## Section 127: Divining Matters of Detention

The Classic says: When the **Heavenly Eye** conceals the **Great One**, military campaigns and the raising of troops will flourish, but for all other matters, it signifies failure. When the **Heavenly Eye** "presses" the **Great One**, objects will be retained, and the **Guest** will "narrow" the **Host** <term>Ke xia zhuren; a configuration where the external force restricts the movement or agency of the self</term>; if they further obtain a **Passing** <gloss>one version reads "Abandoned"</gloss> state, it is entirely inauspicious. If the "Gates" <note>The Eight Gates are a standard feature in Chinese cosmography representing different directions and qualities of energy.</note> are fully prepared, the "Generals" are about to set out, and **Yin and Yang** are in harmony, then all matters <note>The text likely implies that all matters will be successful or proceed smoothly under these harmonious conditions.</note>...

<meta>This page concludes the divinatory instructions for legal matters and fugitive capture discussed on the previous page. It transitions into a generalized framework for interpreting the "Great One's" seasonal strength—categorized into eight states—to determine the outcome of various life events ranging from birth to death.</meta>

In practice, if the situation is the opposite of this, it is inauspicious.

The Method states: Generally, when divining regarding the myriad things of the world, one must use the **Great One's** <term>Taiyi; the supreme cosmic pivot and primary star in this system of divination</term> position within the states of **Resting, Imprisoned, Prosperous, or Assisted** <note>These eight states represent the seasonal strength and "mood" of the cosmic forces, dictating whether an action will succeed or fail.</note> to determine the resolution.

| If the **Great One** stands with a **Prosperous Deity**, it governs new matters | If it stands with an **Assisted Deity**, it speaks of matters of contention and strife | If it stands with a **Fetal Deity**, it governs matters of birth and production | If it stands with a **Drowning Deity**, it governs matters of drowning or submersion | If it stands with an **Imprisoned Deity**, it governs matters of punishment and detention | If it stands with a **Dead Deity**, it governs matters of death and mourning | If it stands with a **Resting Deity**, it governs matters of illness and disease | If it stands with a **Waste Deity**, it governs matters of reform, change, and transformation |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Prosperous** | **Assisted** | **Fetal** | **Drowning** | **Imprisoned** | **Dead** | **Resting** | **Waste** |
| Start of Spring | 8, 3, 4, 9, 2, 7, 6, 1 | Start of Summer | 4, 9, 2, 7, 6, 1, 8, 3 | Start of Autumn | 2, 7, 6, 1, 8, 3, 4, 9 | Start of Winter | 6, 1, 8, 3, 4, 9, 2, 7 |
| Spring Equinox | 3, 4, 9, 2, 7, 6, 1, 8 | Summer Solstice | 9, 2, 7, 6, 1, 8, 3, 4 | Autumn Equinox | 7, 6, 1, 8, 3, 4, 9, 2 | Winter Solstice | 1, 8, 3, 4, 9, 2, 7, 6 |

<note>The numbers in the table refer to the sectors or "palaces" of the divination board. For example, during the Start of Spring, if the Great One is in sector 8, it is considered "Prosperous," but if it is in sector 1, it is considered "Waste."</note>
